Spec:
1. Data (one new migration): campaign_content gets locale (tr|en, NOT NULL; existing rows = the campaign's language, else tr), PK (campaign_id, version, variant, locale). Localized rows store the hash of the primary copy they were adapted from, so the UI can mark them stale after the primary is edited. messages gets locale, set at enqueue from users.locale (frozen like the audience, N-ORC-016). campaigns gets language_mode ('campaign' default | 'recipient'). llm_calls purpose allows 'localize'.
2. Audience: the segment preview and the campaign's audience report consented counts per channel per locale.
3. Localize: POST /api/campaigns/{id}/content/localize {"target_locale":"en"}, streamed like refine. New prompt localize_v1 and schema localize.wire.schema.json. Input: the current version's primary copy of each variant, wrapped as data, never instructions. Output: the same variants in the target language. Prompt rules: adapt, do not translate literally; keep the offer, facts and tone; keep exactly the same {{variables}}; no new URLs, phone numbers or claims; channel limits. Validate each localized copy with content.Validate using the target locale's constraints; same repair loop and budget as content; llm_calls purpose 'localize'; cost_recorded; F7 provider-error mapping. The mock LLM and claudecode support it (the mock returns a deterministic adaptation). The result is a new content version: the primary rows copied plus the localized rows (history kept).
4. Editing: PUT content accepts the copy per variant per locale, validated per locale. Editing the primary makes localized rows stale: the UI shows it and offers "Re-adapt".
5. Approve: new field language_mode. 'campaign' behaves exactly as today. 'recipient' requires, for every sending variant and channel, a valid and non-stale copy for every locale present among the recipients; otherwise 422 missing_localization with details. Idempotency is unchanged.
6. Delivery: when language_mode='recipient', render with the copy for (version, variant, message.locale); otherwise the campaign language as today. Fallback values per message.locale (already the case).
7. Progress API and dashboard: sent/failed per locale per channel (small table).
8. Web: the audience step shows the language breakdown. When the audience has another locale, the content step shows "Write the <Language> version (N recipients)" (streams); variant cards get TR | EN tabs, editable, with a stale notice and "Re-adapt". The approve dialog offers "Everyone in <campaign language>" or "Each recipient in their language" (enabled only when valid, and the default when valid). The dashboard shows the per-language table.
9. Tests:

   - Vitest for the new UI.

   - Acceptance A14: official TR brief -> localize en -> approve recipient mode -> every message to an en user renders the EN copy and every tr user the TR copy; per-locale counts add up. In live mode, semantic checks: language en, valid, differs from TR.
10. Docs: docs/contracts/api.md; DECISIONS D-22 (adapt-after-primary, frozen locale, approve gating, staleness); README (feature, usage, remove B-3 from "Not done"); docs/review/traceability.md B-3 row; observability notes if you add metrics.

package segment

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"time"

	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
)

// CountTimeout is the statement timeout for the dry-run count. 5k seeded users count in milliseconds; the
// timeout only protects the database from a pathological rule (KICKOFF §6.3 step 6).
var CountTimeout = 2 * time.Second

// Count runs the dry-run count with a statement timeout. Reachable means consent for that channel; both
// means consent for both (R-AUD-2). Consent is never part of the rule; it is applied here.
func Count(ctx context.Context, q Querier, v ValidRule) (Audience, error) {
	where, args, err := Compile(v, 1)
	if err != nil {
		return Audience{}, err
	}
	sql := `SELECT count(*),
		count(*) FILTER (WHERE u.sms_consent),
		count(*) FILTER (WHERE u.email_consent),
		count(*) FILTER (WHERE u.sms_consent AND u.email_consent)
		FROM users u WHERE ` + where
	var a Audience
	err = ReadWithTimeout(ctx, q, CountTimeout, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
		return tx.QueryRow(ctx, sql, args...).Scan(&a.Total, &a.SMSReachable, &a.EmailReachable, &a.Both)
	})
	if err != nil {
		return Audience{}, fmt.Errorf("segment: count: %w", err)
	}
	return a, nil
}

// ReadWithTimeout runs the read-only fn in a transaction (a savepoint when q is already a transaction) whose
// statements are limited by `SET LOCAL statement_timeout`. It always rolls back: that undoes the SET LOCAL
// even inside a caller's transaction (a released savepoint would keep it), and fn must not write.
func ReadWithTimeout(ctx context.Context, q Querier, d time.Duration, fn func(pgx.Tx) error) error {
	tx, err := q.Begin(ctx)
	if err != nil {
		return err
	}
	defer func() { _ = tx.Rollback(ctx) }()
	// The value is our own integer, not input; SET does not accept bind parameters.
	if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, fmt.Sprintf("SET LOCAL statement_timeout = %d", d.Milliseconds())); err != nil {
		return err
	}
	return fn(tx)
}

// Package llm is the LLM boundary: the Provider interface that the adapters implement (anthropic, mock,
// claudecode) and the Service that owns prompts, validation, the repair loop and llm_calls accounting, so
// every adapter goes through the same checks (KICKOFF §6). Owned by the llm builder.
//
// "The model proposes, the backend disposes": nothing a provider returns is trusted until it has passed
// strict decoding and the deterministic validators in internal/segment and internal/content.
package llm

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"time"

	"smartcampaign/internal/content"
	"smartcampaign/internal/segment"
)

// ErrNotImplemented marks unfinished code paths.
var ErrNotImplemented = errors.New("llm: not implemented")

// Purpose is recorded in llm_calls.purpose.
type Purpose string

const (
	PurposeSegment Purpose = "segment"
	PurposeContent Purpose = "content"
	PurposeRepair  Purpose = "repair"
	PurposeRefine  Purpose = "refine"
)

// StopReason is the normalized reason the model stopped.
type StopReason string

const (
	StopEndTurn   StopReason = "end_turn"
	StopMaxTokens StopReason = "max_tokens"
	StopRefusal   StopReason = "refusal"
)

// SystemBlock is one block of the system prompt; Cache marks the end of the stable, cacheable prefix.
type SystemBlock struct {
	Text  string
	Cache bool
}

package llm

import (

// MaxRepairs is the number of repair turns after the first attempt (KICKOFF §6.4).
const MaxRepairs = 2

// Default output limits. Adaptive thinking at effort low shares max_tokens with the answer, so the limits
// leave room for it; a max_tokens stop doubles the limit for the next attempt.
const (
	DefaultSegmentMaxTokens = 8192
	DefaultContentMaxTokens = 12288
	maxTokensCeiling        = 32768
)

// Service runs the two LLM tasks end to end.
type Service struct {
	provider       Provider
	recorder       Recorder
	metrics        *metrics.Metrics
	log            *slog.Logger
	segmentTimeout time.Duration
	contentTimeout time.Duration
	now            func() time.Time
}

// Option configures a Service.
type Option func(*Service)

// WithMetrics emits the llm_* metrics of docs/contracts/observability.md.
func WithMetrics(m *metrics.Metrics) Option { return func(s *Service) { s.metrics = m } }

// WithTimeouts sets the per-call timeouts (LLM_SEGMENT_TIMEOUT, LLM_CONTENT_TIMEOUT).
func WithTimeouts(segment, content time.Duration) Option {
	return func(s *Service) {
		if segment > 0 {
			s.segmentTimeout = segment
		}
		if content > 0 {
			s.contentTimeout = content
		}
	}
}

// WithLogger sets the logger; message bodies are never logged.
func WithLogger(l *slog.Logger) Option { return func(s *Service) { s.log = l } }

// WithNow injects the time source used for latency (tests).
func WithNow(now func() time.Time) Option { return func(s *Service) { s.now = now } }

// NewService wires a provider and a recorder (nil = no persistence).

	Language string `json:"language"`
	SMS      struct {
		Text string `json:"text"`
	} `json:"sms"`
	Email struct {
		Subject string `json:"subject"`
		Body    string `json:"body"`
	} `json:"email"`
	Notes string `json:"notes"`

// Content returns the copy in the storage shape.
func (p Proposal) Content() Content {
	return Content{SMSText: p.SMS.Text, EmailSubject: p.Email.Subject, EmailBody: p.Email.Body}
}

// MaxProposalBytes bounds what ParseProposal accepts (5000-rune body ≈ 20 KB at most).
const MaxProposalBytes = 64 << 10

// properties and wrong types are issues with JSON paths, like segment.Parse.

// Claimed is a message this dispatcher now holds a lease on, joined with what rendering needs.
type Claimed struct {
	ID             string
	CampaignID     string
	UserID         int64
	ContentVersion int
	Variant        string // a | b: which row of campaign_content this message renders (api.md §9.3)
	Attempts       int    // value after the claim: the number of this try
	Throttled      int
	Unanswered     int  // earlier tries that got no answer (ambiguous, or lease expired mid-send)
	ConfirmReplays int  // confirmation replays already used (need.md N-DEL-009)
	Skipped        bool // consent was revoked after enqueue: already moved to skipped_no_consent
	Recipient      Recipient
}

// Claim takes up to n due messages. One statement does it all: FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED picks rows no other
// claimer holds (R-SND-3 layer 3), the join re-checks consent at send time (R-AUD-2), and the update moves
// consented rows to `sending` with a lease while unconsented rows go straight to skipped_no_consent.
// Only messages of campaigns in `sending` are due, so a cancelled campaign stops draining.
func (q *Queue) Claim(ctx context.Context, n int) ([]Claimed, error) {
	return q.ClaimOn(ctx, q.Pool, n)
}

// ClaimOn is Claim on a specific connection source.

# need.md — Decisions taken without the owner, and questions for the owner

This file is the asynchronous control surface between the AI agents and the owner. The owner is not always online. Whenever an agent needed a decision and continued without the owner, the decision and the question are lo

Protocol: KICKOFF.md §4.

**How the owner answers:** write one of these in the entry's **Owner response** line:

- `ACCEPT`
- `CHANGE: <instruction>`
- `DISCUSS`

The next agent that owns the area implements the answer and updates the status.

**Status legend:**

| Emoji | Status | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 🟡 | OPEN | Decided provisionally; owner review pending. |
| ❓ | QUESTION | No safe default; needs the owner. |
| 🔁 | CHANGE REQUESTED | Not yet implemented. |
| ✅ | ACCEPTED / RESOLVED | Done. |

// Package store owns the Postgres connection pool and the embedded schema migrations.
package store

import (
	"context"
	"embed"
	"fmt"
	"io/fs"
	"slices"
	"strings"
	"time"

	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5"
	"github.com/jackc/pgx/v5/pgxpool"
)

//go:embed migrations/*.sql
var migrationFS embed.FS

// migrationLockID is the advisory lock key that serializes concurrent `migrate` runs.
const migrationLockID = 7_261_108_001

// MaxConns is the pool size of the API and the worker (S5 in docs/stress/REPORT-api.md measures it under load).
const MaxConns = 20

// Open creates a pool and pings the database once.
func Open(ctx context.Context, url string) (*pgxpool.Pool, error) {
	cfg, err := pgxpool.ParseConfig(url)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("parse DATABASE_URL: %w", err)
	}
	cfg.MaxConns = MaxConns
	pool, err := pgxpool.NewWithConfig(ctx, cfg)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	pingCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, 5*time.Second)
	defer cancel()
	if err := pool.Ping(pingCtx); err != nil {
		pool.Close()
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("ping database: %w", err)
	}
	return pool, nil
}

// OpenWithRetry retries Open until ctx ends, so services survive starting before Postgres is ready.
func OpenWithRetry(ctx context.Context, url string) (*pgxpool.Pool, error) {
	delay := 250 * time.Millisecond
	for {
		pool, err := Open(ctx, url)
		if err == nil {
			return pool, nil
		}
		select {
		case <-ctx.Done():
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("database not reachable: %w", err)
		case <-time.After(delay):
		}
		delay = min(delay*2, 5*time.Second)
	}
}

func migrationNames() ([]string, error) {
	names, err := fs.Glob(migrationFS, "migrations/*.sql")
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	slices.Sort(names) // timestamp prefixes make lexical order the apply order
	return names, nil
}

	if _, err := conn.Exec(ctx, "SELECT pg_advisory_lock($1)", migrationLockID); err != nil {

	}
	defer conn.Exec(context.WithoutCancel(ctx), "SELECT pg_advisory_unlock($1)", migrationLockID) //nolint:errcheck // released on disconnect anyway

	names, err := migrationNames()

	for _, name := range names {
		version := versionOf(name)
		var exists bool
		if err := conn.QueryRow(ctx, "SELECT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM schema_migrations WHERE version = $1)", version).Scan(&exists); err != nil {
			return applied, err
		}
		if exists {
			continue
		}
		sql, err := migrationFS.ReadFile(name)
		if err != nil {
			return applied, err
		}
		err = pgx.BeginFunc(ctx, conn, func(tx pgx.Tx) error {
			// Exec without arguments uses the simple protocol, which allows multiple statements per file.
			if _, err := tx.Exec(ctx, string(sql)); err != nil {
				return err
			}
			_, err := tx.Exec(ctx, "INSERT INTO schema_migrations (version) VALUES ($1)", version)
			return err
		})
		if err != nil {
			return applied, fmt.Errorf("migration %s: %w", version, err)
		}
		applied = append(applied, version)
	}
	return applied, nil
}

// MigrationsApplied reports whether every embedded migration is recorded; /readyz uses it.
func MigrationsApplied(ctx context.Context, pool *pgxpool.Pool) (bool, error) {
	names, err := migrationNames()
	if err != nil {
		return false, err
	}
	versions := make([]string, len(names))
	for i, n := range names {
		versions[i] = versionOf(n)
	}
	var n int
	err = pool.QueryRow(ctx, "SELECT count(*) FROM schema_migrations WHERE version = ANY($1)", versions).Scan(&n)
	if err != nil {
		return false, err
	}
	return n == len(versions), nil
}

func (d *Dispatcher) release(ctx context.Context, log *slog.Logger, c Claimed, delay time.Duration) {
	rctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, 5*time.Second)
	defer cancel()
	if err := d.queue.Release(rctx, c, max(delay, 0)); err != nil {
		log.Warn("release failed; the lease will expire", "err", err)
	}
}

func (d *Dispatcher) template(ctx context.Context, c Claimed) (Template, error) {
	key := c.CampaignID + "/" + strconv.Itoa(c.ContentVersion) + "/" + c.Variant
	if v, ok := d.tmpl.Load(key); ok {
		return v.(Template), nil
	}
	t, err := d.queue.Template(ctx, c.CampaignID, c.ContentVersion, c.Variant)
	if err != nil {
		return Template{}, err
	}
	d.tmpl.Store(key, t)
	return t, nil
}

// zeroDepth resets this channel's queue_depth gauges. The gauge is exported by every worker replica and
// summed or maxed by the dashboards, so a replica that lost the lock must not keep its last reading.
func (d *Dispatcher) zeroDepth() {
	for _, s := range depthStatuses {
		d.Metrics.QueueDepth.WithLabelValues(string(d.Channel), s).Set(0)
	}
}

type sentCall struct {
	Req SendRequest
	At  time.Time
	N   int
}

func (c *scriptedClient) Send(ctx context.Context, r SendRequest) (SendResult, error) {
	c.mu.Lock()
	n := len(c.calls) + 1
	c.calls = append(c.calls, sentCall{Req: r, At: time.Now(), N: n})
	fn := c.fn
	c.mu.Unlock()
	return fn(n, r)
}

func (c *scriptedClient) snapshot() []sentCall {
	c.mu.Lock()
	defer c.mu.Unlock()
	return append([]sentCall(nil), c.calls...)
}

// variantTexts reads the primary rows of one content version, keyed by variant (the localized rows of B-3 are
// read through Campaign.Content). It queries campaign_content itself because a version is one row per variant
// and locale (migrations 20261110230000, 20261111120000).
func variantTexts(ctx context.Context, db DB, id string, version int) (map[string]content.Content, error) {
	rows, err := db.Query(ctx, `SELECT variant, sms_text, email_subject, email_body FROM campaign_content
		WHERE campaign_id = $1 AND version = $2 AND adapted_from IS NULL`, id, version)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}

// generateContent streams the model's JSON as SSE (docs/contracts/api.md §5). Errors before the stream starts
// are plain JSON; afterwards they are an `error` event. Closing the request cancels the model call.
func (s *Server) generateContent(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
	id := r.PathValue("id")
	in, err := s.campaigns.ContentInput(r.Context(), id)
	if err != nil {
		s.fail(w, r, err)
		return
	}
	ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(r.Context(), s.d.ContentTimeout)
	defer cancel()
	sse := newSSE(w)
	p := s.d.LLM.Provider()
	sse.send("meta", map[string]string{"provider": p.Name(), "model": p.Model(), "prompt_version": ContentPromptVersion})
	cv, meta, err := s.campaigns.GenerateContent(ctx, id, in,
		func(d llm.Delta) { sse.send("delta", map[string]string{"text": d.JSONFragment}) },
		func(n llm.RepairNotice) {
			errs := n.Errors
			if len(errs) == 0 {
				errs = json.RawMessage("[]")
			}
			sse.send("repairing", map[string]any{"attempt": n.Attempt, "errors": errs})
		})
	if err != nil {
		code, msg, details := s.describe(r, err)
		sse.send("error", map[string]any{"error": map[string]any{"code": code, "message": msg, "details": details}})
		return
	}
	sse.send("result", map[string]any{"content": cv, "llm": meta})
}

package campaign

import (
	"context"
	"strings"
	"testing"

type rowView struct {
	version     int
	variant     string
	label       string
	source      string
	instruction *string
}

func contentRows(t *testing.T, svc *Service, id string) []rowView {
	t.Helper()
	rows, err := svc.Pool.Query(context.Background(),
		`SELECT version, variant, label, source, instruction FROM campaign_content WHERE campaign_id = $1 ORDER BY version, variant`, id)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	defer rows.Close()
	var out []rowView
	for rows.Next() {
		var r rowView
		if err := rows.Scan(&r.version, &r.variant, &r.label, &r.source, &r.instruction); err != nil {
			t.Fatal(err)
		}
		out = append(out, r)
	}
	return out
}

# web/ — Vue 3 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite

Owned by the web builder. Root rules in `../CLAUDE.md` apply.

- Contracts: `../docs/contracts/api.md` (endpoints, error model, SSE events). Do not invent fields; if the
  contract is missing something, return a `need_entries` item.
- Use context7 for Vue, Vite, Vitest and Playwright docs. Use the `frontend-design` skill for the look;
  clarity beats decoration.
- `npm run dev` serves on 5173 and proxies `/api`, `/healthz`, `/readyz` to `localhost:8080`.
- `npm test` runs Vitest. `npm run build` runs `vue-tsc` and then Vite; the output in `dist/` is embedded
  into the Go API binary (`web/embed_spa.go`, build tag `spa`).
- Character counting must match the server exactly: code points (`[...s].length`), UTF-16 units for UCS-2.
  `../docs/contracts/testvectors/*.json` are run by both Vitest and Go tests. Never edit the vectors here.
- Generation streaming is a POST answered with `text/event-stream`: read it with `fetch` and the SSE parser
  in `src/lib/`. The partial-JSON parser is for display only; the server's final `result` event is canonical.
- The campaign dashboard uses `EventSource` (GET). On reconnect, take the `snapshot` event as the truth.
- Approve stays disabled until the server's validation report says the content is valid for the chosen
  channels. Show every error state in human words (LLM failure, 422, refusal, network).
- Accessibility: every input has a label; everything works with the keyboard.
- TypeScript is pinned to `~6.0.3` on purpose: `vue-tsc` uses the JavaScript compiler API, and TypeScript 7
  is the native port. Do not bump it without checking `vue-tsc` support.

<script setup lang="ts">
import { computed } from 'vue'
import type { Campaign, LlmMeta, ValidationReport, VariantInput, VariantKey } from '../lib/types'
import { variantsOf } from '../lib/variants'
import LlmPanel from './LlmPanel.vue'
import VariantCard from './VariantCard.vue'

// `forms` is the composer's reactive editor state; the cards bind to it directly.
const props = defineProps<{
  campaign: Campaign
  forms: Record<VariantKey, VariantInput>
  hasB: boolean
  checks: Record<VariantKey, { report: ValidationReport | null; key: string; validating: boolean }>
  reportIsCurrent: (k: VariantKey) => boolean
  stream: { active: boolean; repairing: boolean; attempt: number; started: boolean; target: 'all' | VariantKey }
  validating: boolean
  hasUnsavedEdits: boolean
  saving: boolean
  llm: LlmMeta | null
}>()
defineEmits<{ generate: []; stop: []; save: []; next: []; refine: [VariantKey, string] }>()

const keys = computed<VariantKey[]>(() => (props.hasB ? ['a', 'b'] : ['a']))
const current = (k: VariantKey) => (props.reportIsCurrent(k) ? props.checks[k].report : null)

// Approve gating (R-FLOW-3): the button is enabled only when the server's report for the *latest saved*
// content version says every variant that will be sent is valid for every chosen channel, and each chosen
// channel has recipients.
import type { Campaign, Channel, SendVariant } from './types'
import { variantProblem } from './variants'

export interface GateInput {
  campaign: Campaign | null
  channels: Channel[]
  // True when the editor holds text that is not the latest saved version.
  hasUnsavedEdits: boolean
  // True while a save or validation request is in flight.
  busy?: boolean
  // Which variant(s) will be sent (B-2). Defaults to a.
  variant?: SendVariant
  // A reason the chosen send time is not allowed (F5), or '' / undefined when it is fine.
  scheduleProblem?: string
}

export interface Gate {
  enabled: boolean
  // Human reason shown next to a disabled button.
  reason: string
}

const NAMES: Record<Channel, string> = { sms: 'SMS', email: 'email' }

export function approveGate(g: GateInput): Gate {
  const no = (reason: string): Gate => ({ enabled: false, reason })
  const c = g.campaign
  if (!c) return no('Campaign is not loaded yet.')
  if (
    c.status === 'sending' ||
    c.status === 'scheduled' ||
    c.status === 'cancelled' ||
    c.status === 'completed' ||
    c.status === 'completed_with_failures'
  ) {
    return no('This campaign has already been approved.')
  }
  if (!c.segment) return no('Find the audience first.')
  if (c.status === 'draft') return no('Find the audience first.')
  if (g.channels.length === 0) return no('Choose at least one channel.')
  if (!c.content) return no('Generate or write the content first.')
  // After a segment change the campaign drops back to `segmented`: the content must be saved or regenerated.
  if (c.status !== 'content_ready') return no('The audience changed. Regenerate or save the content again.')
  if (g.hasUnsavedEdits) return no('Save your edits first; approval uses the saved version.'

const GENERATE_EVENTS = new Set(['meta', 'delta', 'repairing', 'result', 'error'])

export function toGenerateEvent(raw: RawSseEvent): GenerateEvent | null {
  if (!GENERATE_EVENTS.has(raw.event)) return null
  try {
    return { type: raw.event, data: JSON.parse(raw.data) } as GenerateEvent
  } catch {
    return null
  }
}

// Reads the generation stream. Resolves when the stream ends; rejects with ApiError for pre-stream failures
// (404/409/network). A terminal `error` event is delivered through onEvent like any other event.

  campaignId: string,
  onEvent: (e: GenerateEvent) => void,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<void> {
  return streamPost(generateUrl(campaignId), {}, onEvent, signal)

<script setup lang="ts">
import { computed, ref, watch } from 'vue'
import ApproveStep from '../components/ApproveStep.vue'
import AudienceStep from '../components/AudienceStep.vue'
import BriefStep from '../components/BriefStep.vue'
import ContentStep from '../components/ContentStep.vue'
import ErrorNotice from '../components/ErrorNotice.vue'
import StatusPill from '../components/StatusPill.vue'
import StepNav from '../components/StepNav.vue'
import { useComposer, type Step } from '../lib/composer'
import { links, navigate, replaceHash } from '../lib/router'

const props = defineProps<{ campaignId: string | null }>()
const c = useComposer(props.campaignId)
const approveStep = ref<InstanceType<typeof ApproveStep> | null>(null)

const reachable = computed<Record<Step, boolean>>(() => ({
  brief: true,
  audience: !!c.campaign.value?.segment,
  content: !!c.campaign.value?.segment,
  approve: !!c.campaign.value?.content,
}))

// A campaign that is already sending belongs on the dashboard.
watch(
  () => c.campaign.value?.status,
  (s) => {
    if (c.campaign.value && s && ['scheduled', 'sending', 'cancelled', 'completed', 'completed_with_failures'].includes(s)) {
      navigate(links.live(c.campaign.value.id))
    }
  },
)

// Keep the address bar on the campaign once it exists, so a reload resumes here.
watch(
  () => c.campaign.value?.id,
  (id) => {
    if (id && props.campaignId === null) replaceHash(links.composer(id))
  },
)

async function confirmApprove() {
  const ok = await c.approve()
  approveStep.value?.close()
  if (ok && c.campaign.value) navigate(links.live(c.campaign.value.id))
}

const staleConflict = computed(() => c.error.value?.code === 'stale_content_version')
const alreadyApproved = computed(() => c.error.value?.code === 'already_approved')
</script>

class FakeEventSource {
  static last: FakeEventSource | null = null
  readyState = 1
  onopen: ((e: Event) => void) | null = null
  onerror: ((e: Event) => void) | null = null
  listeners: Record<string, ((e: MessageEvent<string>) => void)[]> = {}
  constructor(public url: string) {
    FakeEventSource.last = this

    ;(this.listeners[type] ??= []).push(l)
  }
  close() {}
  emit(type: string, data: unknown) {
    for (const l of this.listeners[type] ?? []) l({ data: JSON.stringify(data) } as MessageEvent<string>)
  }
}

// End-to-end happy path (KICKOFF §8, P1): the official brief → audience → streamed content → edit → approve →
// dashboard reaches a terminal state. Runs against the real API, worker and mock provider with the mock LLM.
import { expect, test, type Page } from '@playwright/test'

const OFFICIAL_BRIEF =
  'Son 30 günde sepetine ürün ekleyip alışveriş yapmayan İstanbul’daki kullanıcılara %30 indirim hatırlatması gönder. Samimi ama kısa olsun.'

// Samples the SMS field every 25 ms until the stream has finished, and returns the distinct values seen.
// More than one non-empty value means the text arrived in pieces, not in one final paint.
async function watchSmsWhileStreaming(page: Page): Promise<string[]> {
  return page.evaluate(
    () =>
      new Promise<string[]>((resolve) => {
        const seen: string[] = []
        const started = Date.now()
        const timer = setInterval(() => {
          const el = document.querySelector<HTMLTextAreaElement>('#sms-text-a')
          const v = el?.value ?? ''
          if (v && seen[seen.length - 1] !== v) seen.push(v)
          const done = document.body.innerText.includes('Saved as version 1')
          if (done || Date.now() - started > 30_000) {
            clearInterval(timer)
            resolve(seen)
          }
        }, 25)
      }),
  )
}

//go:build acceptance

// Package acceptance drives the product end to end through its HTTP API, the way a marketer's browser does:
// brief → segment → content → approve → delivery, with the real API handler, real delivery dispatchers and
// the mock provider, all in this process, against a throwaway database (docs/contracts/api.md §9.9).
//
//	make acceptance                                  # mock LLM (CI)
//	make acceptance PROVIDER=anthropic <spend flag>  # owner only: real Claude, writes a report
//
// Assertions are semantic (fields used, unsupported list non-empty, consent), never exact model text, so the
// same scenarios run against the mock and the real model.
package acceptance

import (
	"bufio"
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"encoding/json"

# Contract: HTTP API and SSE events

Status: v2 · 2026-11-10 · owner: orchestrator · implements: R-FLOW-1..5, R-OBS-1, R-OBS-3, R-SND-1, R-SND-3,
B-1 (scheduled send), B-2 (A/B variants). v2 adds §9 (phase 2); v1 shapes stay valid unless §9 says otherwise.

Base: same origin as the SPA (`http://localhost:8080`), JSON in UTF-8, no CORS. Timestamps are RFC 3339 UTC.
IDs: campaigns and messages are UUIDs, users are integers.

## 1. Error model

Every non-2xx JSON response:

```json
{ "error": { "code": "validation_failed", "message": "Human-readable summary.",
             "details": [ { "path": "sms_text", "code": "too_long", "message": "…" } ] } }
```

| HTTP | `code` | When |
|---|---|---|
| 400 | `bad_request` | Malformed JSON, missing fields, bad query params |
| 400 | `idempotency_key_required` | Approve without `Idempotency-Key` |
| 404 | `not_found` | Unknown campaign |
| 409 | `invalid_state` | Action not allowed in the campaign's current status |
| 409 | `stale_content_version` | `base_version` / `content_version` is not the latest |
| 409 | `approve_in_progress` | A concurrent approve holds the campaign |
| 409 | `already_approved` | Approved earlier with a different `Idempotency-Key` |
| 422 | `validation_failed` | Brief, rule or content invalid (`details` lists every problem) |
| 422 | `llm_invalid_output` | Model output still invalid after 2 repairs (`details` = last errors) |
| 422 | `llm_refused` | Model refused (`details[0].code` = refusal category or `unknown`) |
| 422 | `content_invalid` | Approve with content not valid for a selected channel |
| 422 | `empty_audience` | Approve when a selected channel has 0 reachable recipients |
| 422 | `variant_missing` | Approve or refine names variant `b` (or `ab`) on a version that has no variant B |
| 422 | `invalid_send_at` | `send_at` outside now + 1 min .. now + 7 days (§9.6) |
| 502 | `llm_failed` | Provider error or timeout after SDK retries |
| 502 | `llm_provider_rejected` | The LLM provider answered 4x

## D-20 · Two variants in one call; refine; honest cost and provider errors [B-2, F3, N-LLM-005, F7]

- **Content v2.** One streamed call returns exactly two variants (`content_v2` wire schema with fixed keys
  `a`/`b`, so "exactly two" is a schema property). Each variant is validated alone with the v1 rules (160
  worst case, subject ≤ 80 single line, variables, language, NUL) and a label of 1–40 code points; any invalid
  variant sends the whole answer through the unchanged repair loop. A version is one `campaign_content` row
  per variant; the API keeps a v1 mirror of variant A. `max_tokens` for content went from 8192 to 12288.
- **Refine.** `refine_v1` rewrites one variant: the current variant goes into the prompt as JSON, the
  instruction as escaped data in its own tag, and the system prompt says it cannot change any rule. The answer
  is validated and repaired like any other; nothing invalid is saved ("ignore the 160 limit" ends valid or
  `llm_invalid_output`). The new version copies the other variant; earlier versions stay. `llm_calls.purpose`
  is `refine` (its repairs `repair`).
- **cost_recorded (owner CHANGE to N-LLM-005).** A failed `llm_calls` insert still does not fail a paid
  generation, but the response says `cost_recorded: false` and the UI shows a non-blocking

**Tool result**

````
# Smart Campaign Composer

A marketer writes a campaign brief in plain language. Claude turns it into a **validated audience segment**
(with live counts from the database) and **streams** SMS and email copy. The marketer edits, previews with the
longest real names, and approves. Messages then go through a **Postgres queue** to a **mock SMS/email provider**
at no more than 100 per second per channel, and a dashboard shows delivery live.

Also built (phase 2, owner decisions 2026-11-10): **A/B variants** with a deterministic 50/50 split (bonus
B-2), **scheduled send** (bonus B-1), a **manual segment-rule editor**, **refine a variant with an
instruction**, **stop sending**, a notice when an LLM call's cost could not be recorded, a clear message when
the AI provider rejects the key or credits, and an end-to-end **acceptance suite** (`make acceptance`).

Go backend · Vue 3 + TypeScript frontend · Postgres (datastore and queue) · synthetic users only · mock
provider only.

- Decisions and their reasons: [`DECISIONS.md`](DECISIONS.md)
- Decision log with the owner's answers: [`need.md`](need.md)
- API, SSE, DSL, provider and metric contracts: [`docs/contracts/`](docs/contracts/)
- How the AI agents were used: [`ai-sessions/README.md`](ai-sessions/README.md)

---

## Quick start

Requirements: Docker with Compose v2. Nothing else is needed for the app and its tests.

### Without an API key (mock LLM)

```bash
docker compose up        # or: make up   (always forces the mock LLM)
```

Open <http://localhost:8080>. The first build takes a few minutes (Go and Node images, multi-stage builds).
Compose runs migrations and seeds 5000 synthetic users, then starts the API, the worker and the mock provider.
With no key the app uses a deterministic **mock LLM** and shows a banner saying so; the whole flow still works.

### With a Claude API key

```bash
cp .env.example .env
  next step for many concurrent campaigns.
- No authentication or multi-tenancy; the app is a single-marketer de
… [4744 more characters truncated]
````

**Tool result**

```
### The demo in five steps

1. **New campaign** — the official example brief is prefilled
   (“Son 30 günde sepetine ürün ekleyip alışveriş yapmayan İstanbul’daki kullanıcılara %30 indirim
   hatırlatması gönder. Samimi ama kısa olsun.”). Click **Find audience**.
2. **Audience** — the rule as the server understood it, in words (Şehir: İstanbul · Son 30 günde sepete ürün
   ekledi · Sepeti terk edilmiş), the counts per channel with consent, Claude's assumptions, and the LLM call
   details (model, latency, tokens, cache read/write, cost, prompt version). **Edit rule** opens an editor
   built from the field catalog (field, operator and value limited to what the field allows); the counts
   refresh as you type, **Save rule** stores it with an **Edited** badge and keeps Claude's original rule.
3. **Content** — **Write with Claude** streams **two variants** (A and B, each with its angle, e.g. friendly
   vs. urgency) side by side. Counters show the length with the longest name and city in this audience, the
   SMS encoding and segments. Edit either card, or type an instruction (“daha kısa”, “more formal”) and
   **Rewrite** one variant; every save or rewrite is a new version.
4. **Approve** — pick channels and **Send A**, **Send B** or **A/B test (50/50)**; **Send now** or
   **Schedule** (1 minute to 7 days ahead, local time). Approval is idempotent and enqueues in one transaction.
5. **Dashboard** — live progress per channel (and per variant), throughput, ETA, provider errors by kind,
   recent attempts, a countdown for a scheduled campaign, and **Stop sending**. The **System** page shows the
   health of every component.

The A/B split is a deterministic hash of (campaign, user), so a user gets the same variant on SMS and email.
The dashboard reports sent/failed per variant; there are **no open or click metrics** (the mock provider has
none), so this is the split and per-variant delivery foundation, not experiment analysis.

Staleness stored (a flag set by every writer) or computed on read.\n- **Decision taken:** (2). The primary row's `locale` records the language at save time and is informational; `adapted_from IS NULL` is what makes it primary, so regenerating the segment in another language (which changes `campaigns.language` but leaves content alone, as before B-3) cannot orphan the primary copy. A localized row whose locale equals the campaign's current language is ignored on read. Staleness is computed on read as `adapted_from ≠ sha256 of the variant's current primary texts` (length-prefixed fields; the label is not part of it), so no writer has to know about it. Migration defaults: existing content rows get the campaign's language, else `tr`; `messages.locale` is back-filled from `users.locale` (the spec said \"set at enqueue\", an old message has no better source); the column defaults stay (`'tr'`, `'campaign'`) so a plain INSERT keeps working. A fresh generation (§5) starts a version without adaptations (new copy, nothing to adapt from); refine and PUT carry adaptations over (stale by hash where the primary changed); the v1 single-variant PUT body drops them, as it drops variant B. ## Results
| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `make test` | exit 0: the Go packages pass under `-race`, plus Vitest (242 tests, 23 files) |
| `make lint` | 0 issues, `vue-tsc` clean |
| `make e2e` | 4 of 4 pass, including the new `localize.spec.ts` |
| `make acceptance` (mock) | 14 of 14 pass, including the new A14 |
| `make sanitize-check` | clean |

No timing test failed in the final runs, so there is nothing to report under V-026.

Two failures along the way, both mine and both fixed:
- **A14, first run:** the render check saw 0 provider requests because my request recorder matched `/sms` instead of `/v1/sms`.
- **e2e:** first the spec used a locator that didn't match the textarea's accessible name, then it read the English copy as if it were the Turkish one. The card stays on the EN tab after localizing, which is intended.

## Commits
The 13 commits since `092b6f8` are:
- `432c74b` store: migration (locale on content and messages, `language_mode`, `localize` purpose)
- `4c2337e` segment: audience count per recipient locale
- `2aaf89d` llm: `localize_v1` prompt, `LocalizeContent` with fidelity checks, mock adaptation
- `d8a82f9` delivery: render the copy and fallback of the message's locale in recipient mode
- `d98d77e` campaign and api: localize endpoint, per-locale edits and staleness, approve gate, per-locale progress
- `914934e` web: language breakdown, TR | EN tabs, Re-adapt, language choice in approve, per-language dashboard table
- `458dece`, `881586d` acceptance A14 and the request-recorder fix
- `8cda431` lint fixes
- `5dbce0c` docs
- `9011b95`, then the spec fix after it: the e2e commits
- the final docs commit: the mock acceptance report with A14

## Not finished, or worth knowing
- **Live-model A14 not run.** Live runs are owner-only; its semantic checks (English, valid, different from TR) are written but only exercised against the mock.
- **API section number.** I put the contract in `docs/contracts/api.md` §9.10, because §9.9 is already the acceptance suite.
- **Fidelity checks.** The backend checks variables, new links or phone numbers, language, length and "not identical to the source". It cannot check that no new claim was added; that stays with the marketer in the EN tab.
- **Not built:**
  - Only `tr` and `en`.
  - Localize needs both variants; a one-variant version from the old `PUT` body is refused with `variant_missing`.
  - There is no "adapt only variant A".

## need.md entries (all OPEN)
- **N-ORC-018:** data model.
  - The primary row is the one with `adapted_from IS NULL`.
  - Staleness is computed on read from a hash of the primary text.
  - Backfill rules for existing rows.
  - A fresh generation starts without adaptations; refine and PUT carry them over.
- **N-LLM-007:** localize decisions.
  - Both variants are required.
  - The extra fidelity checks.
  - The mock adaptation never echoes source text.
- **N-ORC-019:** approve gate and delivery.
  - Per-channel reading of "locales present among the recipients" (please confirm).
  - Fallback names follow `message.locale` in recipient mode only. The spec said that was "already the case", but the worker used the campaign language, so "campaign behaves exactly as today" took precedence.
  - A message with no copy fails as `content_missing` and never falls back to the other language.
- **N-WEB-006:** UI details.
  - "N recipients" counts people of that language before consent.
  - Unsaved primary edits mark the adaptation out of date immediately.
  - `language_mode` is only sent in the approve body for `recipient`.

I also updated D-22, the README, the traceability B-3 row, `observability.md`, and the `purpose` label list in the metrics table.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - The primary row is `adapted_from IS NULL`. Localized rows store `adapted_from` = sha256 hex over the three length-prefixed texts (not the label).
   - Staleness is computed on read, as hash mismatch against the variant's current primary.
   - A localize call saves a new version: primary rows copied plus localized rows. A fresh generation drops adaptations, refine carries them over, a PUT without `localized` carries them over, and the v1 PUT body drops them.
   - Approve gate (recipient mode): per channel, every other locale among that channel's consented recipients needs, per sent variant, a copy that exists, is not stale and is valid for that channel. Otherwise 422 `missing_localization`, with details paths like `variants.a.localized.en` and codes missing | stale | invalid.
   - `messages.locale` is set from `users.locale` at enqueue in both modes.
   - Delivery: `CopyLocale` is empty for the primary copy, else the localized locale. In recipient mode the fallback uses the message locale; in campaign mode it uses the campaign language. A missing copy fails the message as `content_missing`.
   - LocalizeFidelity checks per field: variable sets equal, no new URL/email/phone, not identical to the source.
   - Source copy reaches the model as json.Marshal-escaped data inside `<source_variants>`. The mock never echoes source text.
   - Audience `by_locale`, and progress `locales` (reusing the VariantProgress shape).
   - API section number is §9.10, because §9.9 is the acceptance suite.
   - Web: composer state (`localForms`, `localChecks`, `languageMode` with default and explicit-choice logic), `localization.ts` helpers, approveGate `languageMode`. `language_mode` is sent only for `recipient`.
